Completed means the initial curated cross-reference pass: the source verse and every listed target passage were read in the pinned Saints-Translation English records, and each retained pair has a specific explanation. It does not mean every possible biblical connection has been exhausted, or independent scholarly approval.

1 Kings 1:1

King David was old and advanced in days; and they wrapped him in garments, but he did not grow warm.

  1. 1 Chronicles 23:1 David advanced in days before the succession notices

    Both describe David as old or full of days; Chronicles additionally states that he made Solomon king, whereas Kings begins with his lack of warmth, preserving the different supplied notices.

    Read matching passage

    1 Chronicles 23:1 David was old and full of days, and he made Solomon his son king in his place over Israel.

    Pinned source record

1 Kings 1:2

His attendants said, “Let them seek a young virgin for our lord the king. She shall attend the king and keep him warm; she shall lie with him, and our lord the king will become warm.”

  1. 1 Kings 1:3-4 Same-chapter the proposed attendance and warming arrangement and its narrated outcome

    The attendants propose finding a young woman to attend and warm David; the following account names Abishag and reports her service and warming, without reconstructing a medical diagnosis or adding an unreported marriage.

    Read matching passage

    1 Kings 1:3 And they sought a fair young woman throughout all the territory of Israel, and found Abishag the Shunammite, and brought her to the king.

    1 Kings 1:4 The young woman was very fair; she kept the king warm and served him, but the king did not know her.

  1. Jonah 4:3-8 Prophetic death-request parallel

    Elijah asks the Lord to take his life while beneath a broom bush; Jonah likewise asks the Lord to take his life and later loses heart beneath the sun after his shade withers. The shared death request is compared without equating their different grievances or plants.
